The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.

Permanent support exponent.
Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent
µ > 0, and the normal chart, inverse trees, coarea logarithm, and fixed local atlas have positive exponential
locality exponents
                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.
Choose
                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.
Then all six maps act in the same κ-weighted rooted support space. No per-step weakening of κ is required.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives
                                        X           X
                               τ (P ) ≤   τ (Si ) +    de + Cgeo (n − 1).
                                           i            e∈Tcov


Multiplication by eκτ (P ) leaves the residual connector decay e−(µ−κ)de . Every fixed chart, inverse, coarea,
and atlas branch is summable at exponent κ. Localization costs a polynomial in the rooted radius but
does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
finite fixed-template suprema.

Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).


                                                        35
The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.
   For each fixed local template define the finite constants CW , CHaar , Ccoa , Cchart , CSch as the suprema
on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
uniform under the standing convention.

1. the one-step rooted marked supplier bounds are finite:
                                          Km < ∞,             0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
   uniformly under the standing convention;

   associated-graded diagonal is

   unmarked scalar degree is six;

                                           ∥DΦL (0)|stable ∥ < 1.
No upper bound of the form maxm Km < 4 is required.
Proof. Fix the finite number Γ. Choose D so that
                                                  δD > Γ + 2.
On the finite local quotient, the diagonal eigenvalues have modulus at most L−1 , with the unmarked
scalar sector at most L−2 . Since JL is finite dimensional and triangular, for every ϵL > 0 there is an
equivalent finite-dimensional norm with
                                      ∥JL ∥ ≤ r(JL ) + ϵL ≤ L−1 + ϵL .

                                                 ∥JL ∥ ≤ 2L−1 .

depending only on the fixed D, L, never on volume or weak depth. Since
                                                  δD − Γ > 2,
the short row tends to zero as L → ∞, despite the logarithmic factor, and the long row is O(L−3 ). Choose

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.
   In the canonical squared-coupling normalization,

                                                       11 CA (G) log 2
                                               bAF =                   .
                                                           24π 2
The factor log 2 is the physical scale-two interval. The complete coordinate-intertwining theorem is
printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

5.2     Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

5.2.3 Measure-level intertwining
For each scheduled step let Bj∗ be the exact Karcher pushforward from scale aj to aj+1 , including
canonical rescaling and exact regular/irregular label recombination. Define the corresponding finite-
volume standard-scheme map by
                                     Σj gaj (µ) := gaj+1 (Bj∗ µ).                               (17)
                                               

This evaluates the local renormalization condition after the exact physical pushforward and does not
push a gauge condition through the block. Let Σ∞ = Σstd denote the autonomous rooted-thermodynamic
comparison obtained from the same local functional. The scheduled comparison through the quadratic
jet is O(δG (Mj )u2 + u3 ) + O(u−p e−c/u ) through the two background marks, and becomes OG (u3j ) after
evaluation on an admissible directed orbit.
Theorem 5.6 (Nonautonomous full-measure coordinate intertwining). For the fixed tail-compatible
schedule s and all sufficiently small u,

                             hj+1 ◦ Fj = Σj ◦ hj         for every scheduled step j.                       (18)

The autonomous identity is only the common C 3 endpoint comparison. For another schedule or the
regular-only inner-chart measure, the quadratic-jet comparison is the row of Lemma 5.5. On an admissible
directed orbit it is OG (u3j ) in value and OG (u2j ) after one scalar derivative. No vanishing finite-volume
two-jet and no exponential acoustic wrapping row is asserted.

is Lemma 5.3 and U4. Gauge fixing occurs only inside the local definition of g after equality of the
physical measures, so no global gauge-orbit pushforward identity is used.

Definition 6.36 (Actual family incidence trace space). For a family F, let U = U (F). Cut every physical
carrier line at each pivot in U . In every remaining cycle of the physical carrier skeleton choose the least
closure edge and the least selected pivot on that edge; let Rg (F) be the resulting clean pivot set. Then

                   Rg (F) ∩ U = ∅,        every physical carrier cycle meets U ∪ Rg (F).                  (87)

The actual cut space is                                      O
                                    Hg,F := L2 (GU ) ⊗                 L2 (G)p .                          (88)
                                                           p∈Rg (F )

All activity hyperedges are internal to the first product-group convolution operator in (85); no cross-edge
incidence is declared to be a dimensionless auxiliary flag.
Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.

operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,

   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

                                     Σj gaj (µ) := gaj+1 (Bj∗ µ).                               (17)

                             hj+1 ◦ Fj = Σj ◦ hj         for every scheduled step j.                       (18)

                                        e

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of
HX has zero complete local jet below engineering degree five. In the reflection-even physical background
two-point sector, degree five is absent by signed-hypercubic reflection, so the first nonzero associated-graded
component has degree at least six. The two-quantum-leg kernel is a sum of at most C|X|2 rooted edge-pair

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Definition 5.11 (Lowenstein infrared dimension at the coefficient interface). Give a vector, background-

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected

the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,

  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.4    Ultraviolet supplier closure
The finite-volume normal chart, strict macrostep contraction, and irregular Fréchet flatness used below
are not separate inputs: they are respectively Theorem 2.5, Theorem 4.30, and Theorem 3.31.

                                           s−1

whenever sup ∥DΦ − L∥⋆ < 1 − λ. This change of stable norm does not alter the marginal coordinate or
its dyadic coefficient.
5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff
D, there are normalized source coordinates in the weak regime such that:
   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running
       logarithm;

                                                     r2 L(r)pD

      at physical scale r;

  This theorem stops at the compact first-crossing handoff. It does not invoke the later macrobridge or
matching transfer.
Proof. Normalize each principal block by the fundamental matrix in Lemma 5.25. The strict complemen-

Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M , differen-
tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two

only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest

Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-

contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.

theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.

transform is a self-map of the closed Banach space Airr . Its unique fixed graph and all marked derivatives
remain in that space. This proves the supportwise identity, not merely a cancellation after summing
supports.
Lemma 5.9 (Marked two-quantum-leg reduction in the actual stable fiber). Let H = X HX be the
                                                                                           P
tangent from Theorem 5.8. Choose 0 < a0 < κ and η = (κ − a0 )/2. Then
                                         X
                                     sup    e(a0 +η)τ (X) ∥HX ∥a;3,2 < ∞.
                                        e
                                            X∋e

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of
HX has zero complete local jet below engineering degree five. In the reflection-even physical background
two-point sector, degree five is absent by signed-hypercubic reflection, so the first nonzero associated-graded
component has degree at least six. The two-quantum-leg kernel is a sum of at most C|X|2 rooted edge-pair
terms, with coefficients bounded by C(1 + diam X)q ∥HX ∥a;3,2 .

Proof. The exponential row is the differentiated invariant-graph bound with an unused support reserve.
Equation (19) removes, after the active marks, every local jet carried by Πext ; in particular it removes
the marked B 2 A2 row that invalidates an unmarked centering argument. At first order in the tangent
insertion, the one-loop identity L = I − V + 1 = 1 shows that every additional quantum vertex is
a tree attachment to the unique loop; after those tree attachments are collected, the insertion enters
the background two-point amplitude through one effective two-quantum-leg kernel. Two fluctuation
derivatives choose an ordered pair of edges in X, producing at most C|X|2 terms. Rooted parallel
transport and finite localization cost only a support polynomial. Reflection excludes an odd engineering
degree in the physical scalar two-point sector.
Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Definition 5.11 (Lowenstein infrared dimension at the coefficient interface). Give a vector, background-
vector, ghost, or antighost leg infrared dimension one, and give a lattice difference and the Lowenstein
variable 1 − s infrared dimension one. For a periodic vertex symbol V let νIR (V ) be its lower Taylor order
when all incident soft momenta and 1 − s are scaled together, and put

                               ρ(V ) := νIR (V ) + nA (V ) + nc (V ) + nc̄ (V ),

where background-vector legs are counted in nA . The SI–7M vertex condition used here is

                                                   ρ(V ) ≥ 4                                           (20)

for every elementary or reduced local vertex entering a Lowenstein forest. For a connected soft graph γ
whose massless vector and ghost lines have order −2, incidence and Lγ = Iγ − |Vγ | + 1 give
                                          X                        X

Lemma 5.12 (Complete infrared vertex and forest admission). Fix X with a diam X ≤ 1, a graph in
Lemma 5.10, q ∈ Q⋆ , and at most two background-amplitude marks. With Lowenstein parameter s, write
                                                X
                                  RGLZ
                                       (q, s) =     TFLZ IG (q, s),                            (22)
                                                   F ∈F(G)


where TFLZ is the ordered SI–6B/SI–7M forest operator. At s = 1 only this complete packet is evaluated.
  Every elementary vertex and every reduced vertex admitted by the forest satisfies (20). More explicitly,

                       vertex family                 νIR       number of dimension-one legs    ρ
             vector or ghost quadratic symbol         2                     2                  4
           three-vector or ghost–vector vertex       ≥1                     3                 ≥4
         four-vector, seagull, or tadpole vertex     ≥0                     4                 ≥4
             pure-Wilson reduced local vertex       4−n                     n                  4
           loop-bearing marked reduced vertex       ≥6−n                    n                 ≥ 6.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the
complete list of soft degrees is

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                   ω ghost bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                  ω seagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.


                                                      45
   The pure-Wilson ultraviolet degrees are the SI–6 degrees. By the one-cycle ledger, a mixed contraction
consumes the loop; otherwise the quotient retains a degree-six marked kernel and has ultraviolet excess at
least two. Hence all overall and proper-quotient ultraviolet degrees required by SI–7 are admissible. After
the finite SI–7M sector decomposition, uniformly in the periodic side M and through the two marks, the
grouped packet obeys
                                                       
                                       X
            RGLZ
                 (q, 1; k) ≤ C PX 1 +     dT4 (k, ξ)−2  ,     PX ≤ C(1 + diam X)p ∥HX ∥a;3,2 ,       (23)
                                      ξ∈SG

with the evident omission of PX for a pure-Wilson packet and with a finite soft set SG . A radius-r shell
contains O(M 4 r4 ) lattice momenta, so (23) contributes O(r2 ) after M −4 normalization. The dyadic shell
sum is uniform in M ; deleting a grounded zero mode only decreases it. SI–7 and SI–7M therefore give the
cutoff and s ↑ 1 limits of the complete packet, and the unused rooted support exponent absorbs PX before
the thermodynamic sum.
Proof. The quadratic, cubic, quartic, gauge-fixing, and ghost rows are the finite Taylor rows of the local
Wilson background action and based Faddeev–Popov density. Their derivative and leg counts give the
first four rows of the table. The marked row is Lemma 5.9; the one-cycle classification in Lemma 5.10
shows that forest reduction either removes the loop or leaves that row intact. Equation (21) then proves
the displayed infrared ledger, including the simultaneous two-line soft configuration at a subtraction point.
This verifies the general vertex hypothesis of SI–7M for the original graph and all reduced vertices. The
SI–7M complete-forest conclusion supplies the grouped sector majorant; the explicit degree two gives the
exponent in (23). The shell count and the strict rooted reserve prove the stated uniform limits.
Lemma 5.13 (Finite-support marked power-counting consequence). Let HX satisfy (19). For a diam X ≤
1, the BPHZL-subtracted one-loop nonexceptional background packet has the form
                    (2)
                   IX (a, q⋆ ) = AX (q⋆ ) + O a2 | log a| C(1 + diam X)q1 ∥HX ∥a;3,2 ,         (24)
                                                         

                                       (2)
where AX (q⋆ ) is finite and [log a] P⋆ IX = 0. Supports with a diam X > 1 have rooted total O(aN ) for
every fixed N .
Proof. Fix one support and q ∈ Q⋆ , keeping background, quantum, and ghost momenta separate. Apply
the complete SI–6B/SI–7M packet and the graph classification of Lemma 5.10. Lemma 5.12 verifies, for
every complete packet and through the two consumed background marks, lattice periodicity, denominator
scaling, numerator smoothness, all overall and proper-subgraph degrees, the pure-Wilson/loop-free-
local/loop-bearing marked trichotomy, support-polynomial control, and uniform rooted domination.
SI–7M is therefore applicable to every complete loop-bearing post-contraction packet. Pure-Wilson
proper divergent subgraphs are removed in the SI–6 scheme, while every quotient that still contains
the distinguished stable insertion has irrelevant excess at least two. Lemma 5.9 gives a finite sum of
two-quantum-leg symbols whose complete marked local Taylor jet starts at engineering degree d ≥ 6. The
complete marked Taylor polynomial of the insertion through degree four is zero by (19). Subtractions
may produce local power rows but cannot restore a marginal input exponent; the remaining symbol
carries ad−4 .
   On each fixed unit loop-momentum annulus, the verified SI–7/SI–7M hypotheses give the uniform
marked bound used below. Write the ultraviolet annuli as |k| ≍ 2j with 0 ≤ j ≤ J(a) = O(| log a|). A
marked component of engineering degree d contributes to P⋆ with an explicit ad−4 and an annulus row
bounded by C2(d−4)j . If d > 4, the annulus sum is geometric:
                                                   J(a)
                                                   X
                                         a   d−4
                                                          2(d−4)j = O(1),
                                                   j=0

so it produces a finite local scheme row and no logarithm. A logarithm occurs only at the marginal
exponent d − 4 = 0. Higher external-momentum Taylor coefficients can lower the annulus exponent to
zero, but then they retain the explicit ad−4 ; for d ≥ 6 their logarithmic row is O(a2 | log a|). Hence an
unsuppressed P⋆ log a term would require a nonzero marked degree-four input jet, which is absent. No
assertion that a redundant dimension-four insertion is annihilated support by support is used: such rows
have already been extracted and are not present in HX .
   Finite-support multiplicity is polynomial in diam X; for a diam X > 1, the strict support reserve
dominates every prescribed power of a.

                                                   (2)

Thus the full centered invariant family differs from the canonically normalized Wilson family by one finite,
scale-independent C 2 scheme map through the two consumed background marks.
Proof. The support polynomial in Lemma 5.13 is absorbed by the strict exponent reserve. The finite-
support amplitudes and their two background derivatives may therefore be summed before removing the
cutoff. At the quadratic order in the marginal coordinate needed for the one-step coefficient, apply the
complete SI–6B/SI–7M lattice BPHZL packet, the exhaustive ledger of Lemma 5.10, and the complete
post-contraction admission check of Lemma 5.12 to every mixed Wilson/stable term. A marginal proper
subgraph containing only Wilson vertices is exactly a standard background-field subgraph and is subtracted
in the SI–6 scheme. After all such subgraphs are contracted, every remaining quotient graph contains at
least one stable marked insertion and hence has positive irrelevant excess, at least two in the reflection-even
physical sector. Its overall annulus sum is therefore a power row or an explicitly a2 | log a|-suppressed
row, not an unsuppressed logarithm. Multiple stable insertions only increase that excess. The marked
stable-fiber identity removes every degree-four input row before this forest analysis. Uniform convergence
leaves one finite C 2 local scheme term and no new unsuppressed logarithmic coefficient.
Lemma 5.15 (Scale-independent finite scheme map). Let gW be the local standard coupling on the
canonically rescaled pure Wilson family and let g⋆ be the same local functional on the full centered
invariant family. On the common weak interval there is one C 2 map
                                  s(g) = g + cs g 2 + O(g 3 ),      s′ (0) = 1,
independent of the dyadic lattice spacing, such that
                                                    g⋆ = s(gW )
at both ends of every factor-two step. Consequently
                                            Σstd = s ◦ ΣW ◦ s−1 .
The C 3 -flat irregular complement is included in s; no analyticity at g = 0 is claimed or needed.
Proof. Evaluate the same canonically rescaled nonexceptional projector P⋆ on the two full weak families.
Their tree Hessians coincide. Their first difference is the invariant-graph tangent uH1 , whose vacuum
scalar and complete dimension-four retained projection vanish. Lemma 5.14 proves, rather than assumes,
that the entire exponentially quasi-local insertion family contributes a finite scale-independent transverse
scheme row and no new unsuppressed log a coefficient. SI–6 excludes an independent background
dimension-four counterterm. The inner-chart difference is therefore a finite C 2 unit-tangent redefinition.
U4 adds a C 3 -flat term through the two consumed background marks, which changes neither s′ (0) nor its
quadratic coefficient. Canonical rescaling identifies the profile, gauge fermion, subtraction projector, and
cutoff with the same unit-lattice template at every dyadic scale, so the same map s occurs before and
after the step. The displayed conjugacy follows from the definitions.

  Every elementary vertex and every reduced vertex admitted by the forest satisfies (20). More explicitly,

                       vertex family                 νIR       number of dimension-one legs    ρ
             vector or ghost quadratic symbol         2                     2                  4

           loop-bearing marked reduced vertex       ≥6−n                    n                 ≥ 6.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the
complete list of soft degrees is

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                   ω ghost bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                  ω seagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

are not separate inputs: they are respectively Theorem 2.5, Theorem 4.30, and Theorem 3.31.

the finite-volume value by O(δG (Mj )vj2 + vj3 ) + O(vj−p e−c/vj ) = OG (vj3 ) and gives (27).

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts

                      Z                                   Z

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

5.3    Domain compactness and chart invariance
Let Ba,N be the rooted activity space with norm
                                               X
                                 ∥F ∥a,N = sup   ea|X| ∥FX ∥C N (KX ) ,
                                                  e
                                                      X∋e

where each KX is a compact finite-dimensional product chart and, for every m, only finitely many rooted
connected support shapes of size at most m occur.
Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,
                                 X      ′                        ′
                            sup       ea |X| ∥FX ∥C N −1 ≤ e−(a−a )L M,
                                  e
                                        X∋e
                                       |X|>L

uniformly in F ∈ F.
  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is
uniform, and the compact embedding
                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on
all shapes of size at most L. Letting L increase and using the uniform tail estimate proves relative
compactness in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

The finite-volume normal chart, strict macrostep contraction, and irregular Fréchet flatness used below
are not separate inputs: they are respectively Theorem 2.5, Theorem 4.30, and Theorem 3.31.

whenever sup ∥DΦ − L∥⋆ < 1 − λ. This change of stable norm does not alter the marginal coordinate or
its dyadic coefficient.
5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

E     Invariant tensor spaces through engineering dimension six
The low-dimensional color input can be stated without choosing a basis. The degree-two invariant
polynomial is unique for every simple g. A primitive degree-three invariant occurs only for Ar , r ≥ 2.
Hence the reflection-even dimension-four scalar block is one-dimensional for every G, while the dimension-
six block may contain the additional cubic curvature contraction in type A. The antisymmetric structure


                                                      111
tensor is always present through the bracket but contributes only inside the already retained covariant
derivative and commutator jets.
   The manuscript uses the complete finite quotient generated by curvature, covariant derivatives, metric
and epsilon contractions, invariant color tensors, total-difference classes, and equation-of-motion contacts
through the declared engineering cutoff. No argument relies on a hand-picked SU(2) basis once this
quotient is used.

F      Theorem-level specialization and dependency map
The purpose of this appendix is provenance rather than a new estimate. It identifies every independent
fixed-group input in the register CG , the first proof call at which that dependence enters, and the later
statements that consume it. In particular, the completion proof in Theorem 11.1 does not rely on an
unprinted constant or on a rank-one identity.

   The table also separates the common mechanisms into four logical classes. Replica–BKAR, Kotecký–
Preiss, history compatibility, support reporting, least-label source forests, OS reconstruction, and spectral-
gap promotion are color-free. The full-alias symbol, normal right inverse, optical split, and spacetime

tensors are enlarged finite spaces rather than rank-one identities.
   The rank-one paper [14] is obtained by taking G = SU(2), sG = 1, and a trivial cubic invariant space.
This map is comparative; the present proof does not invoke the rank-one theorem.

This appendix instantiates the finite ledgers for three non-rank-one groups. For a faithful representation

                                                        113
SU(4).    Use the defining faithful representation on C4 :

                                                           1
                                                               
                                      wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 − Re Tr U .
                                                           4

Then dG = 15, CA (G) = 4, sG = 4, and 2sG = 8 > 15/2. The degree-three invariant is present and is
included in the dimension-six jet block. The scale-two coefficient is 11 log 2/(6π 2 ). The charge vanishes
precisely at I4 and is positive away from it, so Theorem 3.4 applies with the representation-normalized
deficit rather than a rank-one raw trace.

                                                          1

G2 . Use the faithful seven-dimensional real fundamental representation, viewed unitarily after complexi-

                                                     1

Then dG = 14, CA (G) = 4, sG = 4, and 2sG = 8 > 7. There is no primitive cubic invariant and the

   For all three examples, the compact ordinary-periodic toron set TG ⊂ G4 is covered by Lemma D.2;
Theorem D.3 then supplies the same color-free full-alias and optical constants. The spectral sum in
Appendix C converges at the displayed orders, while all nonlinear constants remain finite and group
dependent. These are concrete admissions, not hypotheses of the main theorem.

Lemma 6.27 (Fixed-order source upgrade at matching). For every fixed Q < ∞, the matching-stage

The coefficient norm is the fixed projective norm of the finite marked coefficient space.


                                                              63
Lemma 6.28 (Product Fourier–Wiener calculus). For scalar f ∈ AI , convolution by f on L2 (GI ) is
trace class and
                                ∥Kf ∥S1 (L2 (GI )) = ∥f ∥AI .                               (63)
If I ⊂ I ′ , lifting f by independence of the coordinates in I ′ \ I preserves the norm. Pointwise multiplication
satisfies
                                              ∥f g∥AI ≤ ∥f ∥AI ∥g∥AI .                                       (64)
The same inequalities hold coefficientwise in the declared finite projective marked norm. Left/right
translations, inversion, and coordinate permutations are isometries.
Proof. Peter–Weyl decomposes L2 (GI ) into the blocks Vλ ⊗ Vλ∗ . Convolution has block fb(λ) ⊗ I, whose
trace norm is dλ ∥fb(λ)∥S1 ; summing gives (63). A lifted function has only the trivial Fourier block in
every added coordinate, proving isometry. The standard coefficient-product formula, complete contraction
in the unitary Clebsch–Gordan maps, and the projective trace norm give (64). The remaining symmetries
are unitary changes of Peter–Weyl blocks.

Lemma 6.32 (Cutoff-uniform junction row). Every fixed bulk, toron, seam, reflection, or wrapping
junction has operator norm at most one constant J∗ < ∞, independent of the Peter–Weyl cutoff. At
every fixed passive mark order k ≤ 3 + Q, the corresponding coefficientwise constants J∗,k are finite and
cutoff independent.
Proof. In block-first Hilbert normalization, Clebsch–Gordan recouplings are unitary, invariant-channel
maps are orthogonal projections, and translations and inversion are unitary. All remaining maps belong
to a finite bounded-incidence template set. Passive marks differentiate only the fixed local coefficient
maps and are controlled by the fixed-Q marked row.
